The chain rule along $\log\mu_{\ell+1} = k\log\gamma$, $\log\mu_{i+1} = k\log\beta_i$ and $\log\Lambda = \log\beta_L$ gives the product.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:junction}]
(a) $(hUK)K^{-1}V = hUV$, and no other part of the network involves $U$ or $V$.
(b) Fix a hidden state $h_{\ell}$ and apply the change of basis $G$ there (\cref{sec:setup}). The writes into $h_{\ell}$ produce $h_\ell G$ in place of $h_\ell$. A read that sees $h_\ell$ itself, such as the read of the identity units, computes $(h_\ell G)G^{-1}W = h_\ell W$. With pre-RMSNorm reads, a read sees $N(h_\ell G)$, which by Lemma~\ref{lem:rms} equals $N(h_\ell)Q$ exactly when $G = cQ$ with $Q$ orthogonal and $c = 1$ if $\varepsilon > 0$, and is then absorbed by $W \mapsto Q^\top W$. So nothing downstream changes, for every $G$ in $\GL(\dm)$ without normalization, in $\CO(\dm)$ for $\varepsilon = 0$ and in $\Ort(\dm)$ for $\varepsilon > 0$. With biases and without normalization, the affine change $h_\ell \mapsto h_\ell G + g$ is absorbed by $b_{\mathrm{write}} \mapsto b_{\mathrm{write}}G + g$ on the writes and by $(W, b) \mapsto (G^{-1}W,\, b - gG^{-1}W)$ on the reads, since $h_\ell W + b = (h_\ell G + g)G^{-1}W + b - gG^{-1}W$; this gives $\Aff(\dm)$.
(c) The change of basis at the identity units of block $\ell$ multiplies $U_\ell$ on the right and $V_\ell$ on the left. The change at $h_{\ell-1}$ multiplies the reads of block $\ell$, $U_\ell$ included, on the left, and the change at $h_\ell$ multiplies the writes of block $\ell$, $V_\ell$ included, on the right. Multiplications on the left and on the right commute, and every matrix is multiplied on each side by the change at one junction only, so all these changes commute, and a matrix of the skipless network is touched by one hidden state only. The symmetries inside blocks (\cref{sec:setup}) multiply $\Wread$ on the right and $\Wwrite$ on the left, and commute with all of the above.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Skip connections are gauge fixes\texorpdfstring{ (Theorem~\ref*{thm:gauge})}{}}\label{app:prf-gauge}

We recall \cref{thm:gauge} from \cref{sec:gaugefix}. There $\Lambda_\ell = A_{\ell+1}\cdots A_L$, with $\Lambda_L = I$, the map $\Phi(\theta) = \big(E\Lambda_0, (\Lambda_{\ell-1}^{-1}\Wread^{\ell}, \Wwrite^{\ell}\Lambda_\ell)_{\ell=1}^{L}, D\big)$ of \cref{eq:gaugemap} gives parameters of the residual network, and $F^{\mathrm{res}}_{\Phi(\theta)}$ is its function at $\Phi(\theta)$.
\thmGauge*

\para{Overview.} Part (a) is an induction along the network: in the coordinates $h'_\ell = h_\ell\Lambda_\ell$ the identity units become identity skips, and we check that the residual recursion with transformed weights produces exactly these coordinates. Part (b) lifts every direction on the residual side to one with the identity units held fixed, which makes the derivative of $\Phi$ onto, and solves for the changes of basis that relate two parameters with the same image. Part (c) switches off all branches but one, so that the output compares the bases on the two sides of that block, and a branch that is not identically zero forces them to agree. With biases every hidden state also carries a translation, which the skips do not tie (\cref{lem:translations}).

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:gauge}]
\emph{Part (a), with biases and a skip bias.} Merge the identity units, $A_\ell = U_\ell V_\ell$, and write the network as $h_\ell = h_{\ell-1}A_\ell + k_\ell + f_\ell(h_{\ell-1}\Wread^{\ell} + b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{read}})\Wwrite^{\ell} + b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}}$ with $h_0 = xE + b_E$, where $k_\ell \in \R^{1\times\dm}$ is a skip bias ($k_\ell = 0$ is allowed). Every $A_\ell$ is invertible, hence every $\Lambda_\ell$, and $\Lambda_{\ell-1} = A_\ell\Lambda_\ell$. Define new variables and new weights
\begin{gather*}
h'_\ell = h_\ell\Lambda_\ell,\qquad E' = E\Lambda_0,\quad b'_E = b_E\Lambda_0,\qquad
\Wread'^{\ell} = \Lambda_{\ell-1}^{-1}\Wread^{\ell},\quad b'^{\ell}_{\mathrm{read}} = b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{read}},\\
\Wwrite'^{\ell} = \Wwrite^{\ell}\Lambda_\ell,\quad b'^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}} = (b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}} + k_\ell)\Lambda_\ell,\qquad
D' = D .
\end{gather*}
We show by induction that the residual network with primed weights computes $h'_\ell$. For $\ell = 0$, $h'_0 = (xE + b_E)\Lambda_0 = xE' + b'_E$. Assume $h'_{\ell-1} = h_{\ell-1}\Lambda_{\ell-1}$. The residual recursion with primed weights gives
\begin{align*}
&h'_{\ell-1} + f_\ell(h'_{\ell-1}\Wread'^{\ell} + b'^{\ell}_{\mathrm{read}})\Wwrite'^{\ell} + b'^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}}\\
&\qquad= h_{\ell-1}\Lambda_{\ell-1} + f_\ell(h_{\ell-1}\Wread^{\ell} + b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{read}})\Wwrite^{\ell}\Lambda_\ell + (b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}} + k_\ell)\Lambda_\ell,
\end{align*}
and since $\Lambda_{\ell-1} = A_\ell\Lambda_\ell$ the right-hand side equals $h_\ell\Lambda_\ell$. Finally $h'_LD' = h_LD$ because $\Lambda_L = I$. As changes of basis, this is $G_\ell = \Lambda_\ell$ at $h_\ell$ and $K_\ell = U_\ell^{-1}\Lambda_{\ell-1}$ at the identity units of block $\ell$: they send $U_\ell$ to $G_{\ell-1}^{-1}U_\ell K_\ell = I$ and $V_\ell$ to $K_\ell^{-1}V_\ell G_\ell = \Lambda_{\ell-1}^{-1}A_\ell\Lambda_\ell = I$, and the other matrices as above. For vanilla MLP blocks, these changes of basis are those of Proposition~C.1 of \citet{zhao2023symmetries}, transposed to row vectors. Read the network of mixed blocks as a feedforward network whose hidden layers alternate between the hidden states $h_\ell$, with the identity as activation, and the hidden layers of the blocks, with the mixed activation $\tilde f\big(\big[\, u \;\big|\; z \,\big]\big) = \big[\, u \;\big|\; f(z) \,\big]$ of \cref{sec:setup}. The identity passes every matrix in $\GL(\dm)$ (their Example~C.3), and $\tilde f$ passes $\mathrm{diag}(K, I)$ for every $K \in \GL(\dm)$, so their proposition gives these changes of basis $G_\ell$ and $K_\ell$; for other blocks the same computation does. Propagating a change of basis through a skip is thus their change of basis at the identity units, chosen together with the changes of basis at the two hidden states that the skip joins. The forward version takes $G_\ell = \Pi_\ell^{-1}$ at $h_\ell$, with $\Pi_\ell = A_1\cdots A_\ell$ and $\Pi_0 = I$. By the same induction it gives the residual network with embedding $E$, reads $\Pi_{\ell-1}\Wread^{\ell}$, writes $\Wwrite^{\ell}\Pi_\ell^{-1}$, write biases $(b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}} + k_\ell)\Pi_\ell^{-1}$ and readout $\Pi_LD$. Any linear change of variables $h'_\ell = h_\ell K'_\ell$ that yields identity skips must satisfy $K'^{-1}_{\ell-1}A_\ell K'_\ell = I$, so $K'_\ell = \Lambda_\ell K'_L$: two such changes, the forward and the backward version among them, differ by a global change of basis and, when biases are present, by a translation at each hidden state (Lemma~\ref{lem:translations}).

\emph{Part (b).} The identity units carry no biases of their own, since these would only add to the write bias. For parameters $\theta$ of the mixed network with invertible $A_\ell$, the map $\Phi$ of \cref{eq:gaugemap} is the change of variables of part (a) with $k_\ell = 0$, so $F_\theta = F^{\mathrm{res}}_{\Phi(\theta)}$. Every parameter of the residual network is reached: the $\theta$ with the same entries and $U_\ell = V_\ell = I$ has every $\Lambda_\ell = I$, so $\Phi$ returns these entries. The Fr\'echet derivative $\Der_\theta\Phi(\theta)$ is onto at every $\theta$. A direction $\eta'$ of the residual parameters, with the entries $\eta'_E$, $\eta'^{\ell}_{\mathrm{read}}$, $\eta'^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}}$ and $\eta'_D$ at $E'$, $\Wread'^{\ell}$, $\Wwrite'^{\ell}$ and $D'$, is $\Der_\theta\Phi(\theta)\,\eta$ for the direction $\eta$ of $\theta$ that is zero at every $U_\ell$ and $V_\ell$ and has the entries $\eta'_E\Lambda_0^{-1}$ at $E$, $\Lambda_{\ell-1}\eta'^{\ell}_{\mathrm{read}}$ at $\Wread^{\ell}$, $\eta'^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}}\Lambda_\ell^{-1}$ at $\Wwrite^{\ell}$ and $\eta'_D$ at $D$, since along it every $\Lambda_\ell$ stays fixed; the biases are treated in the same way. Changes of basis $G_0, \dots, G_{L-1}$ at $h_0, \dots, h_{L-1}$, with $G_L = I$, and $K_1, \dots, K_L$ at the identity units send $A_\ell$ to $G_{\ell-1}^{-1}A_\ell G_\ell$ and hence $\Lambda_\ell$ to $G_\ell^{-1}\Lambda_\ell$, so they leave $\Phi$ unchanged. Conversely, let $\Phi(\theta') = \Phi(\theta)$, and set $G_\ell = \Lambda_\ell\Lambda'^{-1}_\ell$, so that $G_L = I$, and $K_\ell = U_\ell^{-1}G_{\ell-1}U'_\ell$. Then $E' = EG_0$, $\Wread'^{\ell} = G_{\ell-1}^{-1}\Wread^{\ell}$, $\Wwrite'^{\ell} = \Wwrite^{\ell}G_\ell$ and $D' = D$; $U'_\ell = G_{\ell-1}^{-1}U_\ell K_\ell$ by the choice of $K_\ell$; and $A'_\ell = \Lambda'_{\ell-1}\Lambda'^{-1}_\ell = G_{\ell-1}^{-1}A_\ell G_\ell$ gives $V'_\ell = U'^{-1}_\ell A'_\ell = K_\ell^{-1}V_\ell G_\ell$. So these changes of basis take $\theta$ to $\theta'$. Since $F_\theta = F^{\mathrm{res}}_{\Phi(\theta)}$ and every residual parameter is reached, the two networks compute the same functions. For every batch of inputs, the Jacobian of the mixed network at $\theta$ is the Jacobian of the residual network at $\Phi(\theta)$ times the derivative of $\Phi$, by the chain rule; a matrix times a matrix that is onto has the rank of the first, so the two Jacobians have the same rank, and the two networks the same functional dimension. For learned skips the same argument applies without the $K_\ell$.

\emph{Part (c).} A single global $G$ is a symmetry: the skip carries $h_{\ell-1}G$ to $h_{\ell-1}G$, and the branch becomes $B_\ell(\,\cdot\,G^{-1})\,G$. Conversely, let $G_0, \dots, G_L$ preserve the function for all parameter values; the change of basis conjugates each branch, $B_\ell \mapsto B_\ell(\,\cdot\,G_{\ell-1}^{-1})\,G_\ell$. First set every write $\Wwrite^{k}$, and its bias if there is one, to zero. The network computes $x \mapsto xED$ and the transformed network $x \mapsto xEG_0G_L^{-1}D$, and these agree for all $D$ and $E$ only if $G_L = G_0$. Now suppose $G_0 = \dots = G_{\ell-1} =: G$ and set every write except that of block $\ell$ to zero. Then $h_L = h_{\ell-1} + B_\ell(h_{\ell-1})$ with $h_{\ell-1} = xE$, block $\ell$ reads $h_{\ell-1}GG_{\ell-1}^{-1} = h_{\ell-1}$, and the transformed network outputs $\big(h_{\ell-1}G + B_\ell(h_{\ell-1})\,G_\ell\big)G^{-1}D$. Equality with $\big(h_{\ell-1} + B_\ell(h_{\ell-1})\big)D$ for all $D$ gives $B_\ell(h)\,(G_\ell G^{-1} - I) = 0$ for all $h$ and all parameters of block $\ell$. As the write of block $\ell$ ranges over all matrices, the outputs of a block whose computation does not vanish identically span $\R^{\dm}$, so $G_\ell = G$, and by induction all $G_\ell$ are equal. With pre-RMSNorm reads the argument is the same, with each read transformed by the orthogonal factor of the change of basis (\cref{sec:setup}); when $\varepsilon = 0$ the first step compares only the orthogonal factors, since the readout sees $N(h_L)$, and the second step, in which the write of block $\ell$ can be scaled freely, equates the scales as well.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Translations are not tied]\label{lem:translations}
In a residual network with biases and without normalization, a translation $h_\ell \mapsto h_\ell + g$ of any single hidden state preserves the function once the biases are adjusted. The symmetry group generated by changes of basis, translations and the symmetries inside blocks therefore has dimension $\dm^2 + (L+1)\dm + s$, against $(L+1)(\dm^2 + \dm) + s$ without skips, and the skips remove $L\dm^2$ dimensions, as without biases. Under RMSNorm no translation is a symmetry.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Add $g$ to the write bias of block $\ell$, or to the embedding bias when $\ell = 0$; subtract $g\Wread^{k}$ from the read bias of every block $k > \ell$; subtract $gD$ from the readout bias. The skips carry $g$ forward unchanged, and every downstream read removes it, so the function is unchanged. Each hidden state therefore keeps its own translation with or without skips, and a skip ties only the linear part of $\Aff(\dm)$, which gives the counts. Under RMSNorm a translation changes $N(h)$ by an amount that depends nonlinearly on $h$, which no read can undo.
\end{proof}

The checks give redundancies of 80 without skips and 32 with them ($\tanh$ networks with biases, $\dm = 4$, $L = 3$; \cref{app:checks}), and a translation at a single interior hidden state changes the function by a relative $2\times10^{-16}$.

\subsection{What the skips remove\texorpdfstring{ (Corollary~\ref*{cor:count})}{}}\label{app:prf-count}

We recall \cref{cor:count} from \cref{sec:count}.
\corCount*

\para{Overview.} Part (a) counts copies of $\G$: one per hidden state without skips, by \cref{prop:junction}, and one with them, by \cpref{thm:gauge}{c}. Part (b) bounds the rank of every batch Jacobian by the number of parameters minus the number of independent directions in which the symmetries move the parameters, which is the dimension of the symmetries when they act freely; \cref{lem:free} proves that they do when the embedding has rank $\dm$ and every block has width at least $\dm$. Part (c) turns both bounds into equalities under the equality condition and subtracts.

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering\small
\caption{Parameters, dimension of the symmetries and, under the equality condition, functional dimension of the networks of \cref{sec:setup}, without normalization or biases; $P$ counts the parameters of the skipless network. Each step of the gauge fix removes as many parameters as dimensions of symmetry, while deleting the identity units removes $2L\dm^2$ parameters but only $L\dm^2$ dimensions of symmetry. Given the value for the residual network, the others below it hold at corresponding parameters without further assumption (\cpref{thm:gauge}{b}, \cpref{prop:normalform}{b}).}
\label{tab:ledger}
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\toprule
Network & Parameters & Symmetry & Functional dimension \\
\midrule
skipless & $P$ & $(L+1)\dm^2 + s$ & $P - (L+1)\dm^2 - s$ \\
mixed, with trainable identity units & $P + 2L\dm^2$ & $(2L+1)\dm^2 + s$ & $P - \dm^2 - s$ \\
learned skips & $P + L\dm^2$ & $(L+1)\dm^2 + s$ & $P - \dm^2 - s$ \\
residual & $P$ & $\dm^2 + s$ & $P - \dm^2 - s$ \\
residual, one block fixed & $P - \dm^2$ & $s$ & $P - \dm^2 - s$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\begin{lemma}[Free action]\label{lem:free}
Consider an MLP network, skipless or residual, without normalization or with pre-RMSNorm, with vanilla MLP blocks with $\tanh$, GELU, ReLU or ReLU$^2$ activations, or with GLU blocks, and the symmetries inside blocks of \cref{sec:setup}. If the embedding has rank $\dm$ and every block has width at least $\dm$, then at generic parameters the changes of basis and the symmetries inside blocks act freely. The same holds if the readout has rank $\dm$ and every block has width at least $\dm$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Permutations and sign flips are isolated, so it suffices to consider changes of basis and rescalings close to the identity. Let changes of basis $G_0, \dots, G_L$ and positive rescalings $c_\ell$ of hidden units (for ReLU and ReLU$^2$), of gates and values (for ReGLU) or of values (for the other GLU blocks) fix a generic $\theta$; for $\tanh$ and GELU there are no $c_\ell$. The embedding transforms as $E \mapsto EG_0$, and $E$ has rank $\dm$, so $G_0 = I$. Suppose $G_{\ell-1} = I$. The read of block $\ell$ then transforms as $\Wread^\ell \mapsto \Wread^\ell\,\mathrm{diag}(c_\ell)$, with entries $1$ on the columns that are not rescaled, and no column of a generic $\Wread^\ell$ vanishes, so $c_\ell = \mathbf 1$. The write then transforms as $\Wwrite^\ell \mapsto \Wwrite^\ell G_\ell$, and $\Wwrite^\ell$ has rank $\dm$ because the block has width at least $\dm$, so $G_\ell = I$. By induction only the identity fixes $\theta$. In a residual network the $G_\ell$ are equal (Theorem~\rpref{thm:gauge}{c}) and the same argument applies. The second statement follows by the mirror induction from the readout, $D \mapsto G_L^{-1}D$: rescalings act freely on the rows of a generic write, except that for ReGLU a row fixes only the product $cc'$ of the gate and value scales of its unit; each read, of rank $\dm$ and with generic columns, then forces $G_{\ell-1} = I$ and the remaining scales to be $1$. With RMSNorm the changes of basis lie in $\CO(\dm)$ or $\Ort(\dm)$, and the argument is unchanged. The width condition is needed: at a hidden state of a skipless network that is written and read at rank $m < \dm$, the changes of basis that fix the parameters form a family of dimension $(\dm-m)^2$, so they move the parameters in only $2\dm m - m^2$ independent directions, as \citet[Proposition~C.3]{zhao2023symmetries} compute for one hidden layer.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Attention blocks]\label{rem:attnfree}
The lemma extends to transformers. Attention blocks also carry the per-head symmetries of \cref{sec:setup}, whose full group is characterized by \citet{wang2025gauge} and which are not our subject. Once the input basis is fixed they act freely on $W_{Q,i}$ and $W_{V,i}$, which have full column rank, and the write $W_O$ has rank $\dm$, so the forward induction goes through; in a mixture of experts the writes of all experts together have rank $\dm$. A numerical check on small skipless transformers confirms free action, including cases beyond the reach of the induction (Section~6 of the notebook, \cref{app:checks}).
\end{remark}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:count}]
\emph{Parts (a) and (b).} If a family of transformations of the parameters leaves the function unchanged, then the function does not change to first order along any direction in which the family moves $\theta$, so these directions lie in the kernel of every batch Jacobian at $\theta$, and the functional dimension is at most $P$ minus their number. For a skipless network, Proposition~\ref{prop:junction} gives $L+1$ independent copies of $\G$, one per hidden state, and the directions in which they move $\theta$ are linearly independent of one another and of those of the symmetries inside blocks at generic parameters: the change of basis at $h_{\ell}$ multiplies $\Wwrite^{\ell}$ on the right and $\Wread^{\ell+1}$ on the left, the symmetries inside block $\ell$ multiply $\Wread^{\ell}$ on the right and $\Wwrite^{\ell}$ on the left, and by hypothesis they act freely at generic parameters (Lemma~\ref{lem:free}). The number of independent directions is therefore $(L+1)\dim\G + s$. For a residual network, Theorem~\rpref{thm:gauge}{c} leaves one copy, and the same argument gives $\dim\G + s$.
\emph{Part (c).} Under the hypothesis, the inequalities of part (b) are equalities, so the generic functional dimensions are $P - (L+1)\dim\G - s$ and $P - \dim\G - s$, and the difference is $L\dim\G$, which is $\dm^2$ per skip without normalization, and with RMSNorm $\dm(\dm-1)/2$ when $\varepsilon > 0$ and $\dm(\dm-1)/2 + 1$ when $\varepsilon = 0$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:rms}); with biases the translations are redundant in both networks and the difference is again $L\dm^2$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:translations}). A network with learned skips without normalization has $P + L\dm^2$ parameters and $L+1$ independent copies of $\GL(\dm)$, since each $A_\ell$ absorbs $A_\ell \mapsto G_{\ell-1}^{-1}A_\ell G_\ell$; under the same hypothesis its functional dimension is $P + L\dm^2 - (L+1)\dm^2 - s = P - \dm^2 - s$, the same as the residual network, in agreement with Theorem~\rpref{thm:gauge}{b}.
\end{proof}


\subsection{No absorption inside a block\texorpdfstring{ (Theorems~\ref*{thm:homgated} to~\ref*{thm:relufirst})}{}}\label{app:absorb}

We recall \cref{thm:homgated,thm:relugelu,thm:depthgelu,thm:relufirst} from \cref{sec:oneblock}, for the question \eqref{eq:absorbask}.
\thmAbsorbHom*
\thmAbsorbReLU*
\thmAbsorbDepth*
\thmAbsorbFirst*

\para{Overview.} \Cref{thm:homgated} compares orders. Along rays from the origin a linear skip scales like $\lambda$ and a block that is homogeneous of degree $k \neq 1$ like $\lambda^k$, and near the origin a gated block is of second order, so the two sides of the absorption equation cannot agree (\cref{prop:f2,prop:gated}); comparing Jacobians at the origin extends this to any depth (\cref{cor:depth}). \Cpref{thm:relugelu}{a} is the substantial case. The absorption equation splits into its odd and even parts in $x$. The even part determines the hidden units of the absorbing block up to sign, and for ReLU up to a positive rescaling, through the kinks for ReLU (\cref{thm:f3}) and through linear independence of ridge functions for GELU (\cref{lem:ridge,thm:f4}). The odd part of ReLU and GELU is exactly $z/2$, so the odd part of the equation is linear and reads off the set $S$ of units whose signs flip; conversely, flipping the signs of the units in $S$ absorbs the skip whenever the condition holds. The generic clause holds because the condition is a finite union of algebraic sets of positive codimension (\cref{cor:f5}), and \cref{rem:singular} covers singular $M$. \Cpref{thm:relugelu}{b} counts the extra units that the matching requires (\cref{prop:widthd}). \Cpref{thm:depthgelu}{a} and \ptlink{thm:depthgelu}{b} compare dimensions: a skipless network has a change of basis at every hidden state as a symmetry, and with small writes a residual network is, to first order, one wide block plus a linear map, whose flat directions \cref{lem:unitid} determines (\cref{lem:rankcrit,lem:smallwrites}). \Cpref{thm:depthgelu}{c} adds the parameters of extra units to the same comparison. \Cref{thm:relufirst} compares the ranks of the linear pieces at an input and at its negative.

The arguments show that the function of a block determines its parameters, in the manner used for $\tanh$ networks by \citet{sussmann1992} and \citet{fefferman1994} and for ReLU networks by \citet{buithimai2020functional}, \citet{petzka2020symmetries} and \citet{rolnick2020reverse}: kink alignment for ReLU, ridge-function independence for GELU. For two-layer ReLU networks with a single output, \citet[Section~3]{petzka2020symmetries} already give the corresponding sign flips, show that irreducible networks computing the same function differ only by these flips, permutations and positive rescalings (their Theorem~1), and state the matching criterion for removing a linear term. \Cref{lem:f1} reduces any invertible skip to the identity skip.

\para{Setting.} Throughout, $\Wread \in \R^{\dm \times m}$ and $\Wwrite \in \R^{m \times \dm}$, $\sigma$ acts coordinatewise, $w_i$ is column $i$ of $\Wread$ and $\hat w_i$ is row $i$ of $\Wwrite$, and inputs $x$ are row vectors. For $S \subseteq \{1, \dots, m\}$, $\Wread[{:},S]$ is the submatrix of columns in $S$, $\Wwrite[S,{:}]$ the submatrix of rows in $S$, and $\Pi_S$ the diagonal projector with $(\Pi_S)_{ii} = 1$ exactly when $i \in S$, so that $\Wread\Pi_S\Wwrite = \Wread[{:},S]\Wwrite[S,{:}]$. A family of vectors is \emph{pairwise non-collinear} if no one of them is a scalar multiple of another, and a block is \emph{nondegenerate} if the columns of $\Wread$ are nonzero and pairwise non-collinear and the rows of $\Wwrite$ are nonzero. The question is whether there is a block $B'(x) = \sigma(x\Wread')\Wwrite'$ of the same width, with $\Wread' \in \R^{\dm\times m}$ and $\Wwrite' \in \R^{m \times \dm}$, such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:absorb}
x + \sigma(x\Wread)\Wwrite = \sigma(x\Wread')\Wwrite'\qquad\text{for all } x \in \R^{1\times\dm} .
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[Invertible reduction]\label{lem:f1}
Let $M \in \R^{\dm\times \dm}$ be invertible. Then
\[
xM + \sigma(x\Wread)\Wwrite = \sigma(x\Wread')\Wwrite'\quad\text{for all } x
\]
if and only if
\[
x + \sigma(x\Wread)\Wwrite M^{-1} = \sigma(x\Wread')\Wwrite' M^{-1}\quad\text{for all } x.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Right-multiply by $M^{-1}$, which is invertible.
\end{proof}
Any invertible linear skip therefore reduces to the identity skip, which is the single-block case of Theorem~\rpref{thm:gauge}{a}.

\begin{proposition}[Homogeneous blocks]\label{prop:f2}
Let $B$ and $B'$ be blocks that are positively homogeneous of degree $k \neq 1$, of any widths, and let $M \neq 0$. Then $xM + B(x) = B'(x)$ fails for some $x$.
\end{proposition}
Examples are $\mathrm{ReLU}^2$ and ReGLU blocks without biases, both of degree $2$.
\begin{proof}
\emph{Outline.} We compare the two sides along rays through the origin: a term of degree one and a term of degree $k \neq 1$ cannot agree at every scale.

Suppose it holds for all $x$ and evaluate at $\lambda x$ with $\lambda > 0$: $\lambda xM + \lambda^kB(x) = \lambda^kB'(x)$. Dividing by $\lambda$ gives $xM = \lambda^{k-1}\big(B'(x) - B(x)\big)$, and $\lambda^{k-1} \to 0$ as $\lambda \to 0$ if $k > 1$ and as $\lambda \to \infty$ if $k < 1$. So $xM = 0$ for all $x$, which contradicts $M \neq 0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Gated blocks]\label{prop:gated}
Let $B$ and $B'$ be gated blocks without biases, of any widths, whose gates are differentiable at $0$ and vanish there, and let $M \neq 0$. Then $xM + B(x) = B'(x)$ fails for some $x$.
\end{proposition}
Examples are SwiGLU and GeGLU blocks, which \citet{shazeer2020glu} defines without biases.
\begin{proof}
\emph{Outline.} Near the origin a gated block is of second order in $x$, while $xM$ is of first order.

A unit of a gated block computes $g(u\cdot x)\,(v\cdot x)$. Since $g(0) = 0$ and $g$ is differentiable at $0$, $\lvert g(u\cdot x)\rvert \le c\,\norm{x}$ for small $\norm{x}$, so the unit is $O(\norm{x}^2)$, and so are $B$ and $B'$. If $xM + B(x) = B'(x)$ for all $x$, then $xM = O(\norm{x}^2)$ as $x \to 0$. Along a ray $x = tz$ this reads $t\,zM = O(t^2)$, so $zM = 0$ for every $z$, which contradicts $M \neq 0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Any depth]\label{cor:depth}
Let every block be as in \cref{prop:f2} with $k > 1$ or as in \cref{prop:gated}, of any widths. Let $F$ be a composition of residual blocks $h \mapsto h + B_\ell(h)$ and $H$ a composition of one or more blocks without skips. Then $F \neq H$. With embeddings and readouts, the residual network $x \mapsto F(xE)D$ differs from the skipless network $x \mapsto H(xE')D'$ whenever $ED \neq 0$, also with pre-RMSNorm and $\varepsilon > 0$. Without normalization the same holds for $\tanh$ blocks.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Each such block maps $0$ to $0$ and has Jacobian $0$ there, because it is $O(\norm{x}^k)$ with $k > 1$, respectively $O(\norm{x}^2)$. By the chain rule $F$ has Jacobian $I$ at the origin and $H$ has Jacobian $0$; with embeddings and readouts these become $ED$ and $0$. Under pre-RMSNorm with $\varepsilon > 0$, $N(0) = 0$ and $N$ has Jacobian $I/\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ at $0$, so every block, read through $N$, still has Jacobian $0$ there, and the residual and the skipless network have Jacobians $ED/\sqrt{\varepsilon}$ and $0$ at the origin. For $\tanh$ blocks without normalization, $H$ and $F(h) - h$ are bounded, so $F(xE)D - H(xE')D'$ differs from $xED$ by a bounded function and does not vanish when $ED \neq 0$.
\end{proof}

\para{Parity.} The remaining activations have an odd part that is exactly linear. For ReLU, $\mathrm{ReLU}(z) = |z|/2 + z/2$. For GELU, $\mathrm{GELU}(z) = z\Phi(z)$ with $\Phi$ the standard normal distribution function, and since $\Phi(-z) = 1 - \Phi(z)$,
\[
\mathrm{GELU}(z) = \Psi(z) + z/2,\qquad \Psi(z) := z\big(\Phi(z) - \tfrac12\big),\qquad \Psi(-z) = \Psi(z).
\]
The even parts, $|z|/2$ and $\Psi$, match the hidden units of the two blocks; the odd part, $z/2$, turns a sign flip into a linear term and gives the absorption condition.

\begin{theorem}[Identity absorption for ReLU]\label{thm:f3}
Let $\sigma = \mathrm{ReLU}$, $\dm \ge 2$, and let the block be nondegenerate. Then \eqref{eq:absorb} has a solution if and only if
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cond}
\Wread[{:},S]\,\Wwrite[S,{:}] = -I_{\dm} \quad\text{for some } S.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Outline.} Sufficiency is a construction: flipping the signs of the hidden units indexed by $S$ produces the missing identity term. For necessity we split \eqref{eq:absorb} into its odd and even parts in $x$, use the kinks of the even part to match the hidden units of the two blocks one to one, and read the index set $S$ off the signs of the matching.

\emph{Sufficiency.} Let $F = I_m - 2\Pi_S$, a diagonal sign matrix, and set $\Wread' = \Wread F$, $\Wwrite' = \Wwrite$. Since $|x\Wread F| = |x\Wread|$ coordinatewise,
\begin{gather*}
\mathrm{ReLU}(x\Wread')\Wwrite' = \tfrac12\big(x\Wread F + |x\Wread|\big)\Wwrite,\\
x + \mathrm{ReLU}(x\Wread)\Wwrite = \tfrac12\big(x\Wread + |x\Wread|\big)\Wwrite + x ,
\end{gather*}
and the two agree if and only if $\tfrac12 x\Wread(F - I_m)\Wwrite = x$ for all $x$, that is $-\Wread\Pi_S\Wwrite = I_{\dm}$, which is \eqref{eq:cond}.

\emph{Necessity.} Suppose \eqref{eq:absorb} holds and split both sides into odd and even parts in $x$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:r12}
\Wread'\Wwrite' = \Wread\Wwrite + 2I_{\dm},\qquad\qquad |x\Wread'|\,\Wwrite' = |x\Wread|\,\Wwrite\ \ \text{for all } x .
\end{equation}
\emph{Step 1, alignment of kinks.} The map $x \mapsto |x\Wread|\,\Wwrite$ fails to be differentiable exactly on the union of the hyperplanes $\{x w_i = 0\}$; these are $m$ distinct hyperplanes, each carrying a genuine kink since $\hat w_i \neq 0$. By the second identity in \eqref{eq:r12} the map $x \mapsto |x\Wread'|\,\Wwrite'$ has the same non-differentiability locus. Each of the $m$ kink hyperplanes $\{x : x w_i = 0\}$ of the target needs at least one unit of $B'$ whose kink lies on it, and $B'$ has only $m$ units; so each hyperplane gets exactly one unit, no two units share a hyperplane, and no kinks cancel. After relabeling, $\Wread' = \Wread C$ with $C = \mathrm{diag}(c_1, \dots, c_m)$, all $c_i \neq 0$.
\emph{Step 2, coefficients.} Substituting, $|x\Wread C| = |x\Wread|\,|C|$, so $|x\Wread|\,|C|\,\Wwrite' = |x\Wread|\,\Wwrite$ for all $x$. For $\dm \ge 2$ and pairwise non-collinear columns, the functions $|x w_i|$ are linearly independent modulo linear functions, because their kink hyperplanes are distinct; hence $|C|\,\Wwrite' = \Wwrite$ and $\Wwrite' = |C|^{-1}\Wwrite$.
\emph{Step 3, signs.} The first identity in \eqref{eq:r12} becomes $\Wread C|C|^{-1}\Wwrite = \Wread\Wwrite + 2I_{\dm}$. With $\Sigma = C|C|^{-1} = \mathrm{diag}(\mathrm{sign}\,c_i)$ this reads $\Wread(\Sigma - I_m)\Wwrite = 2I_{\dm}$; the diagonal of $\Sigma - I_m$ has entries $0$ or $-2$, so with $S = \{i : c_i < 0\}$ we get $\Wread\Pi_S\Wwrite = -I_{\dm}$, and $|S| \ge \dm$ by the rank of $I_{\dm}$.
\end{proof}

The GELU case needs one classical fact about ridge functions, whose short proof we include.

\begin{lemma}[Vanishing sums of ridge functions]\label{lem:ridge}
Let $a_1, \dots, a_r \in \R^{\dm}$ be nonzero and pairwise non-collinear, and let $g_1, \dots, g_r : \R \to \R^k$ be $r-1$ times continuously differentiable with $\sum_{i} g_i(a_i\cdot x) = 0$ for all $x \in \R^{\dm}$. Then every $g_i$ is a polynomial of degree at most $r-2$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix $i$. For each $j \neq i$ choose $u_j \in \R^{\dm}$ with $u_j\cdot a_j = 0$ and $u_j\cdot a_i \neq 0$, which exists because $a_i$ is not a multiple of $a_j$. The directional derivative $\partial_{u_j}$ annihilates every function of $a_j\cdot x$ and maps a function of $a_k\cdot x$ to a function of $a_k\cdot x$. Applying the product of the commuting operators $\partial_{u_j}$, $j \neq i$, to the sum therefore leaves $\prod_{j \neq i}(u_j\cdot a_i)\, g_i^{(r-1)}(a_i\cdot x) = 0$. Since $a_i \neq 0$, $a_i\cdot x$ takes every real value, so $g_i^{(r-1)} = 0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Identity absorption for GELU]\label{thm:f4}
Let $\sigma = \mathrm{GELU}$ and let the block be nondegenerate. Then \eqref{eq:absorb} has a solution if and only if \eqref{eq:cond} holds.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Outline.} The proof follows that of Theorem~\ref{thm:f3}. Linear independence of ridge functions replaces the kink argument, and one extra step shows that matched hidden units have the same scale.

\emph{Sufficiency.} With $F = I_m - 2\Pi_S$, $\Wread' = \Wread F$ and $\Wwrite' = \Wwrite$, evenness of $\Psi$ gives $\Psi(x\Wread F) = \Psi(x\Wread)$, so
\begin{align*}
\mathrm{GELU}(x\Wread')\Wwrite' &= \Psi(x\Wread)\Wwrite + \tfrac12 x\Wread F\Wwrite \\
&= \mathrm{GELU}(x\Wread)\Wwrite - x\Wread\Pi_S\Wwrite \\
&= \mathrm{GELU}(x\Wread)\Wwrite + x .
\end{align*}
\emph{Necessity.} Splitting \eqref{eq:absorb} into even and odd parts gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:g12}
\Psi(x\Wread')\Wwrite' = \Psi(x\Wread)\Wwrite\ \ \text{for all } x,\qquad \Wread'\Wwrite' = \Wread\Wwrite + 2I_{\dm} .
\end{equation}
\emph{Step 1, alignment of directions.} $\Psi$ is analytic and not a polynomial. Zero columns of $\Wread'$ contribute $\Psi(0) = 0$; group the other columns of $\Wread'$ and the columns of $\Wread$ into classes of collinear vectors. In the first identity of \eqref{eq:g12} the terms of each class form one ridge function in the direction of the class, and these ridge functions sum to zero, so each is a polynomial by Lemma~\ref{lem:ridge}. The class of a column $w_i$ of $\Wread$ contains no other column of $\Wread$, so if it contained no column of $\Wread'$ its ridge function would be $-\Psi(x w_i)\,\hat w_i$, which is not a polynomial because $\hat w_i \neq 0$. Hence each of the $m$ classes of the columns of $\Wread$ contains a nonzero column of $\Wread'$, and since $\Wread'$ has only $m$ columns, after relabeling $\Wread' = \Wread C$ with $C = \mathrm{diag}(c_i)$, $c_i \neq 0$.
\emph{Step 2, unit scale.} Collecting the terms of direction $w_i$ on both sides gives, for each $i$, a ridge function $g_i(x w_i) := \Psi(|c_i|\,x w_i)\,(\Wwrite')_{i,:} - \Psi(x w_i)\,(\Wwrite)_{i,:}$ with $\sum_i g_i(x w_i) = 0$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:ridge} each $g_i$ is a polynomial. For $t \to +\infty$, $\Psi(t) = t/2 - t\,\bar\Phi(t)$ with $\bar\Phi(t) = 1 - \Phi(t)$ exponentially small, so $g_i(t) = \tfrac{t}{2}\big(|c_i|(\Wwrite')_{i,:} - (\Wwrite)_{i,:}\big) + o(1)$; a polynomial with this behavior is linear, and it is even because $\Psi$ is even, so it is constant, and letting $t \to 0$ shows the constant is zero. Hence $|c_i|(\Wwrite')_{i,:} = (\Wwrite)_{i,:}$ and the exponentially small remainder $|c_i|\,t\,\bar\Phi(|c_i|t)\,(\Wwrite')_{i,:} - t\,\bar\Phi(t)\,(\Wwrite)_{i,:}$ must vanish, which with $(\Wwrite)_{i,:} \neq 0$ forces $\bar\Phi(|c_i|t) = \bar\Phi(t)$ for all $t > 0$, so $|c_i| = 1$. Therefore $|C| = I_m$ and $\Wwrite' = \Wwrite$.
\emph{Step 3, signs.} Identical to the ReLU case: the second identity in \eqref{eq:g12} becomes $\Wread(C - I_m)\Wwrite = 2I_{\dm}$ with $C = \mathrm{diag}(\pm1)$, so $S = \{i : c_i = -1\}$ satisfies \eqref{eq:cond} and $|S| \ge \dm$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:evenpart}
Any activation whose even part $\psi$ is analytic and not a polynomial matches the hidden units in the same way: Steps~1 and~2 of the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:f4} apply with $\psi$ in place of $\Psi$ (zero columns of $\Wread'$ now contribute a constant, which is a polynomial), except that the unit scale in Step~2 follows from a general fact: if $\sum_k a_k\psi(\alpha_kt)$ is a polynomial for distinct $\alpha_k > 0$, then every $a_k = 0$. Indeed, the Taylor coefficients $\psi_n\sum_k a_k\alpha_k^n$ vanish for all large $n$, infinitely many $\psi_n$ are nonzero, and a generalized Vandermonde matrix with distinct positive nodes is invertible. Linear independence of the ridge functions alone does not suffice: for $\sigma(z) = z/2 + \lvert z\rvert^3$ the even part is homogeneous, so a unit can be rescaled together with its output row, and the unit scale is not forced. The odd part $o$ of $\sigma$ enters only through the flips: with the units matched, both directions reduce to $x = -2\sum_{i \in S} o(xw_i)\,\hat w_i$, with $S$ the flipped units. For $o(z) = z/2$, that is $\sigma(z) - \sigma(-z) = z$, as for SiLU, whose even part is $\tfrac z2\tanh\tfrac z2$, this is \eqref{eq:cond}, and $o(z) = \alpha z$ with $\alpha \neq 0$ only rescales it; a polynomial $o$ of higher degree adds the condition that the units in $S$ cancel its terms of degree three and higher, and a smooth $o$ that is not a polynomial admits no $S$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:ridge}), so the skip is never absorbed at the same width.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Singular linear skips]\label{rem:singular}
Nothing in the proofs of Theorems~\ref{thm:f3} and~\ref{thm:f4} uses that the linear term is the identity. With $xM$ in place of $x$ on the left of \eqref{eq:absorb}, the odd part of the equation reads $\Wread'\Wwrite' = \Wread\Wwrite + 2M$, the even part is unchanged, and the sign step yields $\Wread\Pi_S\Wwrite = -M$. So for any $M \neq 0$, invertible or not, absorption holds if and only if $\Wread[{:},S]\Wwrite[S,{:}] = -M$ for some $S$, with $|S| \ge \operatorname{rank}M$, which is a measure-zero condition on the weights for each fixed $M$. This gives \cpref{thm:relugelu}{a} for singular $M$.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Generic disjointness for one block]\label{cor:f5}
Let $\sigma$ be ReLU or GELU and $\dm \ge 2$. For generic weights, \eqref{eq:absorb} has no solution.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Outline.} The condition of Theorems~\ref{thm:f3} and~\ref{thm:f4} is a finite union of algebraic sets of positive codimension, and such a union has measure zero.

For each of the finitely many $S$, \eqref{eq:cond} is $\dm^2$ polynomial equations in the weights and defines a set of measure zero; the hypotheses of Theorems~\ref{thm:f3} and~\ref{thm:f4} hold outside another set of measure zero. So a generic residual block is not a block of the same width without a skip, and, by the same argument with the two sides of \eqref{eq:absorb} exchanged, a generic block without a skip is not a residual block of the same width: neither class contains the other.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[$\dm$ more units]\label{prop:widthd}
Let $\sigma$ be ReLU or GELU, $\dm \ge 2$ and $M \in \R^{\dm\times\dm}$ invertible.\\
\textup{(i)}\ptlabel{prop:widthd:i} If $\Wread$ has rank $\dm$, a block $B'$ of width $m + \dm$ satisfies $xM + \sigma(x\Wread)\Wwrite = B'(x)$ for all $x$.\\
\textup{(ii)}\ptlabel{prop:widthd:ii} For generic weights no block of width less than $m + \dm$ does.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Outline.} Two units with opposite reads produce a linear term of rank one, so $\dm$ extra units produce $xM$. Conversely, the matching of the units in Theorems~\ref{thm:f3} and~\ref{thm:f4} shows that every extra unit adds at most one such term.

(i) As $\sigma(z) - \sigma(-z) = z$, a column $w$ and rows $a$, $b$ give $\sigma(xw)(a + b) + \sigma(-xw)(-b) = \sigma(xw)\,a + (xw)\,b$. Choose $\dm$ units whose columns $w_{i_1}, \dots, w_{i_{\dm}}$ form an invertible matrix $W_K$, and let $b_1, \dots, b_{\dm}$ be the rows of $W_K^{-1}M$. Adding $b_k$ to the row $\hat w_{i_k}$, and a unit with column $-w_{i_k}$ and row $-b_k$, for each $k$, gives a block of width $m + \dm$ that computes $\sigma(x\Wread)\Wwrite + xW_KW_K^{-1}M$.

(ii) Outside a set of measure zero the block is nondegenerate, and for each of the finitely many sets $S$ of units the determinant of $M + \Wread[{:},S]\Wwrite[S,{:}]$ is nonzero, since it is a polynomial in the weights equal to $\det M \neq 0$ at $\Wwrite = 0$. Let a block $B'$ of width $m'$, with columns $w'_j$ and rows $\hat w'_j$, satisfy $xM + B(x) = B'(x)$. Its odd part gives $\Wread'\Wwrite' = \Wread\Wwrite + 2M$, and its even part $|x\Wread'|\,\Wwrite' = |x\Wread|\,\Wwrite$ for ReLU and $\Psi(x\Wread')\Wwrite' = \Psi(x\Wread)\Wwrite$ for GELU. Group the nonzero columns $w'_j$ and the columns $w_i$ into classes of collinear vectors, write $w'_j = s_ju$ with a unit vector $u$ of the class, $u = w_i/\norm{w_i}$ in the class of $w_i$, and let $P_u = \norm{w_i}\hat w_i$ there and $P_u = 0$ in a class without a column of $\Wread$. The even part then forces, class by class, $\sum_j |s_j|\,\hat w'_j = P_u$ for ReLU, since the functions $|xu|$ of distinct classes are linearly independent (Step~2 of Theorem~\ref{thm:f3}), and for GELU, by Lemma~\ref{lem:ridge} and the independence of the functions $\Psi(\alpha t)$ for distinct $\alpha > 0$ modulo polynomials (\cref{rem:evenpart}), $\sum_{|s_j| = \alpha}\hat w'_j = \hat w_i$ if $w_i$ lies in the class and $\alpha = \norm{w_i}$, and $0$ otherwise. With $Q_u = \sum_j s_j\hat w'_j$ the odd part reads $\sum_u u\,(Q_u - P_u) = 2M$. Call a class mixed if it contains $w'_j$ of both signs, for GELU of both signs and equal $|s_j|$. A class that is not mixed has $Q_u = P_u$ or $Q_u = -P_u$; let $S$ collect the units $i$ whose class is not mixed and has $Q_u = -P_u \neq 0$. Then
\[
2\big(M + \Wread[{:},S]\Wwrite[S,{:}]\big) = \sum_{u\ \mathrm{mixed}} u\,(Q_u - P_u) .
\]
Every class of a column $w_i$ contains some $w'_j$, since $P_u \neq 0$, and every mixed class contains at least two, so $m' \ge m$ plus the number of mixed classes. If $m' < m + \dm$, the right side has rank less than $\dm$, so the left side is singular, which the weights exclude.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:homgated}]
Part (a) is \cref{prop:f2} for homogeneous blocks and \cref{prop:gated} for gated ones, and parts (b) and (c) are \cref{cor:depth}. For part (d), $B$ and $B'$ are bounded for $\tanh$ blocks and $xM$ is not, which gives (a), and (b) and (c) are again \cref{cor:depth}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:relugelu}]
Part (a) is \cref{thm:f3,thm:f4}, with \cref{lem:f1} for invertible $M$ and \cref{rem:singular} for singular $M$; for each $M$ the condition holds only on a set of weights of measure zero (\cref{cor:f5,rem:singular}). Part (b) is \cref{prop:widthd}.
\end{proof}

\para{Depth.} \Cref{thm:depthgelu} and \cref{cor:resdepth} rest on two lemmas. The first turns a gap in functional dimension into disjointness. The second shows that with small writes the Jacobian of a residual network reaches the largest rank that its symmetries allow, by the argument of \cref{thm:onelayer} for one block.

\begin{lemma}[A rank criterion]\label{lem:rankcrit}
Let $\theta \mapsto F_\theta$, $\theta \in \R^P$, and $\eta \mapsto G_\eta$, $\eta \in \R^{P'}$, be two families of networks with the same inputs and outputs, whose outputs are real-analytic in the parameters. Let a Lie group act smoothly on $\R^{P'}$ without changing $G_\eta$, with orbits of dimension at least $k$ at generic $\eta$. If at some $\theta_0$ the Jacobian of $\theta \mapsto (F_\theta(x))_{x \in X}$ has rank greater than $P' - k$ on some finite batch $X$, then for almost every $\theta$ no $\eta$ satisfies $F_\theta = G_\eta$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $F_X(\theta), G_X(\eta) \in \R^n$ for the outputs on $X$. At generic $\eta$ the map $G_X$ is constant along an orbit of dimension at least $k$, so its Jacobian has rank at most $r_0 = P' - k$; the rank is lower semicontinuous, so the bound holds at every $\eta$. The Jacobian of $F_X$ reaches its largest rank $r^*$, which exceeds $r_0$ by hypothesis, outside the zero set of the sum of the squares of its minors of size $r^*$. This function is analytic and not identically zero, so its zero set has measure zero. Take $\theta$ outside it. By the constant rank theorem, $F_X$ maps a neighborhood $U$ of $\theta$ onto an embedded manifold $Y$ of dimension $r^*$, and after shrinking $U$ a linear projection $\pi : \R^n \to \R^{r^*}$ maps $Y$ diffeomorphically onto an open set. The map $\pi \circ G_X$ has rank at most $r_0 < r^*$, so all its values are critical, and by Sard's theorem its image has measure zero. So $G_X(\R^{P'}) \cap Y$ has measure zero in $Y$, and so does its preimage under $F_X$ in $U$, which contains every $\theta'$ in $U$ with $F_{\theta'} = G_\eta$ for some $\eta$. Countably many such $U$ cover the complement of the zero set.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Small writes]\label{lem:smallwrites}
Let $\dm \ge 2$, let $E$ and $D$ have rank $\dm$, and let each block, in any order and of any width, be an attention block with heads of width at most $\dm$, causal or not, on sequences of at least two tokens, or an MLP block, with all MLP blocks $\tanh$, all GELU, all SwiGLU or all GeGLU blocks. At generic parameters, multiply every write, and every write bias, by $\tau$. For all small $\tau \neq 0$ the residual network without biases has functional dimension $P - \dm^2 - s$ without normalization and $P - \dm(\dm-1)/2 - s$ under pre-RMSNorm with $\varepsilon > 0$. Without normalization and attention blocks, biases lower the first value by $(L+1)\dm$. Without an embedding, a readout, biases and normalization it has functional dimension $P - s$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write the write of block $\ell$, for an attention block its output projection, as $\tau V_\ell$. Let $W = [\,\Wread^{1} \mid \dots \mid \Wread^{L}\,]$ place the reads of the MLP blocks side by side and $V$ stack their $V_\ell$, so that $f(nW)V$ is one wide block that holds all units of the network, and let the heads of all attention blocks form one attention block. Divide the columns of the Jacobian that belong to the reads, for attention blocks the queries, keys and values, and to the read biases by $\tau$, which keeps its rank, and let $\tau \to 0$. On a batch of inputs the limit is a linear map $J_0$ on $\R^P$; as in \cref{app:onelayer}, a direction $\eta$ is flat if $J_0\,\eta = 0$ on every input.

\emph{MLP blocks without normalization.} The stream is $h_\ell = xE + b_E + O(\tau)$, so the output is
\[
y = xED + b_ED + b_D + \tau\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\Big(f\big((xE + b_E)\Wread^{\ell} + b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{read}}\big)V_\ell + b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}}\Big)D + O(\tau^2),
\]
with the write biases written as $\tau b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}}$. Hence $J_0\,\eta = \Der_\phi G(\phi, x)\,\psi$ for the one-block function $G$ of \cref{lem:unitid} at $M = ED$, $\gamma = b_ED + b_D$, the columns $c_i$ of $EW$, the entries $\beta_i$ of $b_EW + b_{\mathrm{read}}$ and the rows $a_i$ of $VD$, in the direction $\psi$ with
\begin{gather*}
\psi_M = \eta_ED + E\eta_D,\qquad \psi_\gamma = \eta_{b_E}D + b_E\eta_D + \eta_{b_D} + \textstyle\sum_\ell \eta_{b^{\ell}_{\mathrm{write}}}D,\\
\psi_C = E\eta_W,\qquad \psi_\beta = b_E\eta_W + \eta_{b_{\mathrm{read}}},\qquad \psi_A = \eta_VD,
\end{gather*}
where $\psi_C$, $\psi_\beta$ and $\psi_A$ collect the $\psi_{c_i}$, $\psi_{\beta_i}$ and $\psi_{a_i}$, and the values of the gated units enter as the gates do. At generic parameters the hypotheses of \cref{lem:unitid} hold, as for one block (\cref{app:onelayer}). For a flat $\eta$ the lemma gives $\psi_M = 0$, $\psi_\gamma = 0$ and a rescaling at every unit. As $E$ and $D$ have rank $\dm$, $\psi_M = 0$ gives $\eta_E = EX$ and $\eta_D = -XD$ for some $X \in \R^{\dm\times\dm}$; $\psi_C$ and $\psi_A$ make $\eta_W$ and $\eta_V$ a rescaling of the units, which fixes $\eta_{b_{\mathrm{read}}}$ through $\psi_\beta$; and $\psi_\gamma = 0$ fixes $\eta_{b_D}$ once $\eta_{b_E}$ and the $L$ write biases are chosen. So the flat directions form a space of dimension $\dm^2 + s$, and $\dm^2 + (L+1)\dm + s$ with biases. Without an embedding and a readout, $M = I$ is fixed, and only the rescalings are left.

\emph{Attention blocks without normalization.} Without biases, $y = xED + \tau\sum_\ell g_\ell(xE)D + O(\tau^2)$, where $g_\ell$ is block $\ell$ with the write $V_\ell$. So $J_0\,\eta$ is the derivative of the residual network with one block that holds all heads and units, in the direction with the entries $\psi_M$, $\psi_C$ and $\psi_A$ above and, for each head $i$,
\[
\psi_{B_i} = E\big(\eta_{Q,i}W_{K,i}^\top + W_{Q,i}\eta_{K,i}^\top\big)E^\top/\sqrt{d_h},\qquad \psi_{C_i} = E\big(\eta_{V,i}W_{O,i} + W_{V,i}\eta_{O,i}\big)D,
\]
at $B_i = EW_{Q,i}W_{K,i}^\top E^\top/\sqrt{d_h}$ and $C_i = EW_{V,i}W_{O,i}D$ as in \cref{app:onelayer}, where $W_{O,i}$ holds the rows of $V_\ell$ that belong to head $i$ of block $\ell$. On inputs whose tokens all equal $x$, head $i$ gives every token $xC_i$, and its derivative is $x\psi_{C_i}$, since all its scores move alike. There the equation is that of the units with the linear part $M + \sum_iC_i$, and \cref{lem:unitid} gives a rescaling at every unit. The units then drop out on every input. On inputs whose tokens other than the second equal the first, head $i$ gives the second token the output of \cref{eq:twotok} with $k = 1$ if its block is causal and $k = T - 1$ otherwise; the constants $-\tfrac12\log k$ act as biases of the $\tanh$ units, which \cref{lem:unitid} allows, so Step~2 for attention blocks in \cref{app:onelayer} gives $\psi_M = 0$, $\psi_{B_i} = 0$ and $\psi_{C_i} = 0$. Cancelling $E$ on the left and $E^\top$ or $D$ on the right, the last two leave, as in Step~1 there, the directions of the changes of basis between the queries and keys and between the values and outputs of each head. So the flat directions form a space of dimension $\dm^2 + s$, and without an embedding and a readout of dimension $s$.

\emph{Under pre-RMSNorm.} The stream is $h_\ell = xE + O(\tau)$, every block reads $N(xE) + O(\tau)$, and the output is
\[
y = N(xE)D + \tau\,\Der N(xE)\,\Big(\textstyle\sum_\ell g_\ell\big(N(xE)\big)\Big)D + O(\tau^2),
\]
with $N$ applied to every token. So $J_0\,\eta = \Der N(h)\,\big(x\eta_E + k(n)\big)D + N(h)\eta_D$, with $h = xE$, $n = N(h)$ and $k(n)$ the derivative of the wide block along $\eta$ at the input $n$. This is the equation of the proof of \cpref{thm:onelayer}{b} with $h_1 = xE$ and $\Der_\theta h_1\,\eta = x\eta_E + k(n)$, and its Steps~1 to~5 go through, with $k(n)$ bounded in Step~2 since $n$ stays in the ball, and with the units handled first, through equal tokens, in Step~4. In Step~3 the direction $\eta^Y$ now has the entries $EY$ and $-YD$ at $E$ and $D$ and no others, as $\Der N(h)\,(hY) = N(h)Y$ for antisymmetric $Y$. So the flat directions are these, the rescalings of the units and the changes of basis inside the heads, a space of dimension $\dm(\dm-1)/2 + s$.

In each case the kernel of $J_0$ shrinks as the batch grows and becomes stationary at the flat directions, so on some finite batch $J_0$ has rank $P$ minus this dimension. By lower semicontinuity the Jacobian of the residual network on this batch has at least this rank for all small $\tau \neq 0$. Its symmetries bound the rank by the same number, since they move the parameters in independent directions when $E$ has rank $\dm$ and the queries and values of every head have full column rank: the global change of basis (\cpref{thm:gauge}{c}), the translations (\cref{lem:translations}), the rescalings of the units and the changes of basis inside the heads.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:depthgelu}(a) and (b)]
By \cref{lem:smallwrites}, a residual network without biases has functional dimension $P - \dm^2 - s$ at some parameters, where $s$ counts the changes of basis inside the heads and the rescalings of the values of gated units, and a network of GELU blocks without an embedding and a readout has functional dimension $P$. In a skipless network of the same widths, with embedding $E'$ and readout $D'$, the changes of basis at $h_0$ and $h_L$, those inside the heads and the rescalings have orbits of dimension $2\dm^2 + s$ at generic parameters, where $E'$ and $D'$ have rank $\dm$ and the queries and values of every head have full column rank. As $P - \dm^2 - s > P - 2\dm^2 - s$, \cref{lem:rankcrit} gives the claim. Without an embedding, a readout and attention blocks, for $L \ge 2$ the changes of basis at $h_1$ have orbits of positive dimension, since every block has at least one unit, so \cref{lem:rankcrit} applies again. Under pre-RMSNorm with $\varepsilon > 0$ the networks stay analytic, \cref{lem:smallwrites} gives the residual network functional dimension $P - \dm(\dm-1)/2 - s$, and the orthogonal changes of basis at $h_0$ and $h_L$, which commute with the normalization, have orbits of dimension $\dm(\dm-1) + s$ in the skipless network; as $\dm \ge 2$, \cref{lem:rankcrit} applies once more.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:depthgelu}(c) and (d)]
Let the residual network have blocks with $m_1, \dots, m_L$ hidden units and $P$ parameters. A skipless network whose blocks have $m'_\ell \ge \dm$ hidden units has $P' = P + 2\dm\sum_\ell(m'_\ell - m_\ell)$ parameters, and its changes of basis at $h_0, \dots, h_L$ have orbits of dimension $(L+1)\dm^2$ at generic parameters (\cref{lem:free}). By \cref{lem:smallwrites} the residual network has functional dimension $P - \dm^2$ at some parameters, which exceeds $P' - (L+1)\dm^2$ exactly when $\sum_\ell(m'_\ell - m_\ell) < L\dm/2$. For each of the finitely many such choices of widths, \cref{lem:rankcrit} gives a set of weights of measure zero, and so does their union. For (d), \cpref{thm:relugelu}{b} with $M = I$ turns each residual block, whose read has rank $\dm$, into a block without a skip with $\dm$ more units, and composing these gives a skipless network that equals the residual one.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:relufirst}]
MLP blocks act on each token separately, so we compare the networks on rows $x \in \R^{1\times d_{\mathrm{in}}}$. Outside a set of weights of measure zero, $E$ and $D$ have rank $\dm$, no read of a unit vanishes, and $I + \Wread^{\ell}[{:},S]\,\Wwrite^{\ell}[S,{:}]$ is invertible for every block $\ell$ and every set $S$ of its units, since each determinant is a polynomial in the weights that equals $1$ when the writes vanish. On an open set of inputs on which no unit of the residual network switches, with active sets $S_\ell$, the network is the linear map $x \mapsto xEMD$ with $M = \prod_\ell\big(I + \Wread^{\ell}[{:},S_\ell]\,\Wwrite^{\ell}[S_\ell,{:}]\big)$ invertible, of rank $\dm$. By induction over the blocks, on such a set the pre-activation of each unit of the next block is $x$ times a nonzero vector, so the inputs at which some unit switches have measure zero, and the inputs near which the network is linear of rank $\dm$ form an open dense set $\Omega$.

Let a skipless network $F'$ have embedding $E'$ and a first block with reads $w'_1, \dots, w'_{m'}$, $m' < 2\dm$, and set $a_i = E'w'_i$. Take $x_0$ with $x_0, -x_0 \in \Omega$ and $x_0a_i \neq 0$ whenever $a_i \neq 0$. The units active near $x_0$, those with $x_0a_i > 0$, and those active near $-x_0$ are disjoint, so one of the two sets, say the first after replacing $x_0$ by $-x_0$ if needed, has fewer than $\dm$ units. Near $x_0$ the other units of the first block output zero, so $F'$ depends on $x$ only through the numbers $xa_i$ of the active units, and $F'(x_0 + u) = F'(x_0)$ for every small $u$ in a subspace of dimension greater than $d_{\mathrm{in}} - \dm$. The residual network $F$ changes by $uEMD$, which vanishes only on a subspace of dimension $d_{\mathrm{in}} - \dm$. So some small $u$ changes $F$ and leaves $F'$ unchanged, and $F' \neq F$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{cor:resdepth}]
\emph{Part (a).} Let $r$ be $P$ minus the dimension of the symmetries of the residual network, the value in \cref{lem:smallwrites}. At generic parameters the symmetries move the parameters in independent directions, so the rank of the Jacobian is at most $r$ on every batch. \Cref{lem:smallwrites} gives parameters and a finite batch on which it equals $r$. On this batch the rank is therefore at least $r$ outside the zero set of the sum of the squares of the minors of size $r$, an analytic function that is not identically zero, so this zero set has measure zero. At generic parameters the functional dimension is thus $r$, which is the equality condition.

\emph{Part (b).} When every block has width at least $\dm$, the functional dimension of the skipless network is at most $P - (L+1)\dim\G - s$, and $(L+1)\dm$ lower with biases (\cpref{cor:count}{b}, \cref{rem:attnfree}, \cref{lem:translations}); with biases, the induction in the proof of \cref{lem:free} also makes the translations act freely, through the entries at $b_E$ and at the write biases. This gives the gain of at least $L\dim\G$, with equality when the skipless network satisfies the equality condition.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{cor:exactgain}]
By \cpref{cor:resdepth}{a} the residual network satisfies the equality condition, so it remains to show it for the skipless network; the gain is then $L\dm^2$, with or without biases.

\emph{Part (a), $\tanh$.} With read biases $b_\ell$ and write biases $c_\ell$, which may vanish, the skipless network computes
\[
x^0 = x,\qquad x^\ell = \tanh\big(x^{\ell-1}A_\ell + \beta_\ell\big)\ \ (1 \le \ell \le L),\qquad y = x^LA_{L+1} + \beta_{L+1},
\]
with $A_1 = EW_1$, $A_\ell = V_{\ell-1}W_\ell$ and $A_{L+1} = V_LD$, where $W_\ell$ and $V_\ell$ are the read and the write of block $\ell$, and $\beta_1 = b_EW_1 + b_1$, $\beta_\ell = c_{\ell-1}W_\ell + b_\ell$ and $\beta_{L+1} = c_LD + b_D$. As $\sigma(z) = \tanh(z/2)$ is injective, two such networks have the same output exactly when the nets of \citet{fefferman1994} with the hidden layers $x^\ell = \sigma(2x^{\ell-1}A_\ell + 2\beta_\ell)$ and the output layer $\sigma(x^LA_{L+1} + \beta_{L+1})$ do, and then so do, for every input coordinate and every output, the nets with one input and one output that keep only these. At generic parameters every entry of every $A_\ell$ is nonzero, and no ratio of the weights that one unit sends to two units of a layer of width $m$ is a fraction $p/q$ with $1 \le q \le 100m^2$. These conditions are open, and they are those of his Theorem~I.B.9, on nets with one input and one output, which puts no condition on the thresholds. So near generic parameters, two networks with the same output give, for every input coordinate and every output, nets with one input and one output that agree up to permutations of the units of each hidden layer and sign flips. These are the identity: at the first layer where a unit is moved or flipped, the weights it receives would move by a fixed amount, since none vanishes and the weights that one unit sends to two units differ in absolute value. So the two networks have the same $A_\ell$ and $\beta_\ell$. When $E$, $D$ and every read and write have rank $\dm$, the parameters with the same $A_\ell$ and $\beta_\ell$ are those reached by the changes of basis at the $L+1$ hidden states, and with biases by the translations there.

Take generic parameters and a batch on which the rank $r$ of the Jacobian equals the functional dimension. Near these parameters the rank on this batch stays $r$, so its kernel is the space of directions that are flat for every input, and the parameters with the same outputs on the batch form a manifold of dimension $P - r$ along which the function does not change. So $P - r$ is at most the dimension of the symmetries, and the symmetries give the reverse inequality (\cpref{cor:count}{b}, \cref{lem:translations}).

\emph{Part (b), GELU.} Let block $\ell$ have $m_\ell + \dm$ units, with $m_\ell \ge \dm$, and take a residual network with blocks of $m_\ell$ units at generic parameters, with every write multiplied by $\tau$ as in \cref{lem:smallwrites}. By the proof of \cpref{prop:widthd}{i}, each of its blocks equals a block without skip with $\dm$ more units: for $\dm$ units $i$ whose reads $w_i$ form an invertible matrix, add the row $\gamma_i$ of its inverse to the write of unit $i$, and add a unit with the read $-w_i$ and the write $-\gamma_i$. This construction is smooth in the residual parameters near these, where the chosen reads stay invertible, and gives a skipless network that computes the residual one. By the chain rule, the columns of the skipless Jacobian therefore span those of the residual Jacobian, which, with the read columns divided by $\tau$, tend to the limit $J_0$ of \cref{lem:smallwrites}, of rank $P_R - \dm^2$ on some finite batch, where $P_R = P - 2L\dm^2$ counts the parameters of the residual network. As $\sigma(z) - \sigma(-z) = z$ gives $\sigma'(-z) = 1 - \sigma'(z)$, and every block is the identity at $\tau = 0$, the reads of the added units give the further columns
\[
\big(\sigma'(xEw_i) - 1\big)(xE\delta_i)\,\gamma_iD,\qquad \delta_i \in \R^{\dm},
\]
in the limit. Suppose that $J_0\,\eta$ plus a sum of these vanishes for all $x$. Each added term has the Gaussian part of the term of $\psi_{c_i}$ in \cref{lem:unitid}, with $E\delta_i$ in place of $\psi_{c_i}$ and the row $\gamma_iD$ in place of $a_i$. In the proof of that lemma for GELU, the coefficient of $t^2$ for the slowest unit $j$ becomes $u_j\big((n\psi_{c_j})\,a_j + (nE\delta_j)\,\gamma_jD\big)$. At generic parameters $\gamma_jD$ is not parallel to $a_j$, so $nE\delta_j = 0$ for all $n$ in an open set, and $\delta_j = 0$ as $E$ has rank $\dm$; the proof then goes on as before. So, on some finite batch, the added reads raise the rank in the limit by $L\dm^2$, to $P_R - \dm^2 + L\dm^2 = P - (L+1)\dm^2$. By lower semicontinuity the skipless network has this rank for small $\tau$, which is the most its symmetries allow, and an analytic network keeps its largest rank at generic parameters.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[ReLU-type blocks]\label{rem:reludepth}
For ReLU, ReLU$^2$ and ReGLU blocks without normalization, the proof of \cref{lem:smallwrites} goes through on a finite batch on which no pre-activation of the limit vanishes: \cref{lem:unitid} covers these units, and for small $\tau$ every pre-activation keeps its sign on the batch, so the Jacobian is polynomial in the weights near these parameters. The residual network therefore has functional dimension $P - \dm^2 - s$ on an open set of weights that contains all small enough multiples of generic writes, and at almost every point of this set the skips add at least $L\dm^2$ when every block has width at least $\dm$. The count fails on another open set. For $\dm = 2$, the block $h \mapsto h + 2\big(\mathrm{ReLU}(-h_1), \mathrm{ReLU}(-h_2)\big) = \big(\lvert h_1\rvert, \lvert h_2\rvert\big)$ maps the plane onto a quadrant, and a unit of the next block with the read $-(1, 1)^\top$ never switches on. Its read and its write then leave $2\dm - 1$ more directions flat, and small changes of the weights keep this. Which weights keep the count is left for future work.
\end{remark}

\subsection{No absorption in attention\texorpdfstring{ (Proposition~\ref*{prop:attention}, Lemma~\ref*{lem:heads}, Corollary~\ref*{cor:match})}{}}\label{app:attention}

We recall \cref{prop:attention} from \cref{sec:oneblock}; primes mark the weights and the attention maps of $B'$.
\propAttention*

\para{Overview.} Two kinds of input suffice. If all tokens are equal, attention returns them unchanged, so the value--output products of all heads must add up to $M$. If one token is zero, all its scores are zero, so every head gives it the average of the tokens, while the skip gives it zero; this asks the same sum to vanish. Part (b) makes the conflict quantitative, and analyticity turns it into part (a). With an embedding and a readout, the same two inputs give part (c).

\begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:attention}]
The difference $B'(h) - B(h)$ is one attention block with $H + H'$ heads,
\[
B'(h) - B(h) = \sum_{k=1}^{H+H'} R_k(h)\, h\, C_k,
\]
where the $R_k(h)$ are the $P_j'(h)$ and the $P_i(h)$, and the $C_k$ are the $W_{V,j}'W_{O,j}'$ and the $-W_{V,i}W_{O,i}$. Let $C = \sum_k C_k$.

\emph{Step 1: equal tokens.} Let every row of $h$ be $x$. Each row of $R_k(h)$ sums to $1$, so every row of $R_k(h)h$ is $x$. Hence every row of $F(h)$ is $x(M - C)$.

\emph{Step 2: one zero token.} Let the last row of $h$ be $0$ and the other rows $y$. Every score in the last row of $R_k(h)$ is a product with the last row of $h$, which is $0$. So that row of $R_k(h)$ is $(1/T, \dots, 1/T)$, also with a causal mask, since the last token sees all $T$ tokens. Hence the last row of $F(h)$ is $-\frac{T-1}{T}\,yC$.

\emph{Proof of (b).} Over $\norm{x}, \norm{y} \le 1$, Step 1 gives rows of norm up to $\norm{M - C}$, and Step 2 up to $\frac{T-1}{T}\norm{C} \ge \frac12\norm{C}$. If $\norm{M - C} < \norm{M}/3$, then $\norm{C} > \frac23\norm{M}$, so Step 2 reaches more than $\norm{M}/3$.

\emph{Proof of (a).} $F$ is real-analytic, because the softmax is, and by (b) some entry of $F$ is not identically zero. The zeros of a real-analytic function that is not identically zero form a set of measure zero: in one variable they are isolated, and the general case follows by induction on the dimension with Fubini's theorem.

\emph{Proof of (c).} Let $C_B$ and $C_{B'}$ be the sums of the value--output products of $B$ and $B'$. On inputs whose rows all equal a row vector $u$, Step 1 gives the rows $uE(I + C_B)D$ in the residual network and $uE'C_{B'}D'$ in the network without a skip. On inputs whose last row is $0$ and whose other rows equal $v$, Step 2 gives the last rows $\frac{T-1}{T}\,vEC_BD$ and $\frac{T-1}{T}\,vE'C_{B'}D'$. If the two networks are equal, then $E(I + C_B)D = E'C_{B'}D' = EC_BD$, so $ED = 0$.
\end{proof}

We recall \cref{lem:heads} and \cref{cor:match} from \cref{sec:oneblock}.
\lemHeads*

\para{Overview.} Move one token along a complex line. Each head becomes a meromorphic function of one complex variable, whose poles lie on a vertical line that its score matrix determines, so heads with distinct score matrices have disjoint poles. At a pole of one head the other terms are regular, so its residue must vanish, and the residues force its value--output product to vanish. What is left is the skip and a uniform head, which two simple inputs separate.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:heads}]
\emph{One moving token.} Fix $h$ in the open set, with rows $x_1, \dots, x_T$, and fix $v \in \R^{1\times\dm}$. Replace the second row by $x_2 + tv$ with $t \in \mathbb{C}$, and look at the first row. For a head with score matrix $\Gamma$, the first row has scores $z_r = x_1\Gamma x_r^\top$ for $r \neq 2$, and $a + bt$ for $r = 2$, where $a = x_1\Gamma x_2^\top$ and $b = x_1\Gamma v^\top$. Its output is
\[
g_\Gamma(t) = \frac{e^{a+bt}\,(x_2 + tv) + Z\bar x}{e^{a+bt} + Z}, \qquad Z = \sum_{r\neq 2} e^{z_r}, \qquad \bar x = \frac1Z\sum_{r\neq 2} e^{z_r}x_r,
\]
where $\bar x$ is the average of the other tokens under the weights of the head. The first row of $hM$ is $x_1M$, which does not depend on $t$.

\emph{Poles.} If $b \neq 0$, then $g_\Gamma$ is meromorphic on $\mathbb{C}$, with simple poles at
\[
t_n = c + \frac{(2n+1)\pi i}{b}, \quad n \in \mathbb{Z}, \qquad\text{where } c = \frac{\log Z - a}{b},
\]
so all its poles lie on the vertical line $\operatorname{Re} t = c$. The residue at $t_n$ is $(x_2 + t_nv - \bar x)/b$. If $\Gamma = 0$, then $g_\Gamma$ is affine in $t$ and has no poles.

\emph{The poles tell the heads apart.} For $h$ and $v$ outside a closed set with empty interior, $b \neq 0$ for every $\Gamma_k \neq 0$, and distinct $\Gamma_k \neq 0$ give distinct lines $\operatorname{Re} t = c$. The first condition fails only on the zero sets of nonzero polynomials. For the second, suppose that two heads $\Gamma \neq \Gamma'$ have $c = c'$ for all $h$ and $v$ near some point. Clearing denominators and comparing the terms bilinear in $(x_2, v)$ gives $x_1\Gamma' = \lambda\, x_1\Gamma$ for some number $\lambda \neq 0$. Comparing the terms linear in $v$ then gives $\log Z' = \lambda \log Z$, that is,
\[
\log \sum_{r\neq 2} e^{\lambda z_r} \;=\; \lambda \log \sum_{r\neq 2} e^{z_r}.
\]
Because $T \ge 3$, the sum has at least two terms, $r = 1$ and $r = 3$, and $z_3$ varies with $x_3$. Differentiating in $z_3$ gives $\mathrm{softmax}(\lambda z)_3 = \mathrm{softmax}(z)_3$ on an interval of $z_3$, which forces $\lambda = 1$. So $x_1\Gamma' = x_1\Gamma$ for all $x_1$ near a point, and $\Gamma' = \Gamma$, a contradiction. With two tokens the sum has the single term $r = 1$, and this step fails (\cref{sec:oneblock}).

\emph{Residues.} The identity holds for real $t$ near $0$, so by analytic continuation it holds on all of $\mathbb{C}$ away from the poles. At a pole $t_n$ of a head $\Gamma_k \neq 0$, no other term is singular, so the residue times $C_k$ vanishes: $(x_2 + t_nv - \bar x)\, C_k = 0$ for every $n \in \mathbb{Z}$. Subtracting two consecutive ones gives $vC_k = 0$. This holds for all $v$ in an open set, so $C_k = 0$.

\emph{The skip.} What remains is $hM + P_0(h)\,h\,C_0 = 0$, where $C_0$ is the coefficient of $\Gamma = 0$ (or $0$ if no $\Gamma_k$ vanishes). This is linear in $h$, so it holds for all $h$. Take the first row of $h$ equal to $x$ and the other rows $0$: the first two rows give $x(M + C_0/T) = 0$ and $xC_0/T = 0$. So $C_0 = 0$ and $M = 0$.
\end{proof}

\corMatch*

\begin{proof}[Proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:match}]
Group the heads of $B' - B$ by score matrix, add their value--output products ($W_{V,j}'W_{O,j}'$ for $B'$, $-W_{V,i}W_{O,i}$ for $B$), and apply \cref{lem:heads}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{One free block per hidden state\texorpdfstring{ (Proposition~\ref*{prop:normalform})}{}}\label{app:prf-normalform}

We recall \cref{prop:normalform} from \cref{sec:skipless}, where $W_1$ is an invertible $\dm \times \dm$ block formed by $\dm$ columns of a matrix that reads $h_\ell$, or by $\dm$ rows of a matrix that writes it.
\propNormalForm*

\para{Overview.} The proposition spends the change of basis of \cref{prop:junction} at each hidden state on one square block of a read or of a write: the change of basis given by that block, or by its orthogonal factor under normalization, turns it into the identity or into a triangular matrix.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:normalform}]
By \cref{prop:junction}, a change of basis $G \in \G$ at $h_\ell$ preserves the function and replaces every read $W$ of $h_\ell$ by $G^{-1}W$ and every write $W$ into $h_\ell$ by $WG$. So the columns of a read that form $W_1$ become $G^{-1}W_1$, and the rows of a write that form $W_1$ become $W_1G$. Without normalization $\G = \GL(\dm)$, and $G = W_1$ for a read, or $G = W_1^{-1}$ for a write, turns $W_1$ into $I$. Under RMSNorm with $\varepsilon > 0$, $\G = \Ort(\dm)$ (\cref{lem:rms}). For a read, let $W_1 = QT$ be a QR factorization, with $T$ upper triangular; $G = Q$ gives $Q^\top W_1 = T$. For a write, let $W_1^\top = Q_0R$ be a QR factorization; then $Q = Q_0^\top$ is orthogonal, $T = R^\top$ is lower triangular, $W_1 = TQ$, and $G = Q^\top$ gives $W_1Q^\top = T$. The identity fixes all $\dm^2$ entries of $W_1$, and the triangular form fixes the $\dm(\dm-1)/2$ entries on one side of its diagonal. The change of basis at $h_\ell$ touches only the writes into $h_\ell$ and the reads of $h_\ell$, and a matrix is touched on each side by one hidden state only (\cpref{prop:junction}{c}), so the reductions at all hidden states can be made together, one block per hidden state. In a residual network, one change of basis $G \in \G$ applied at every hidden state preserves the function (\cpref{thm:gauge}{c}); it replaces every read $W$ by $G^{-1}W$ and every write $W$ by $WG$, and leaves the identity skips unchanged, since $G^{-1}IG = I$. The same choice of $G$ then turns one block $W_1$, of any one layer, into the identity or into a triangular matrix.
\end{proof}

\subsection{LayerNorm reduces to RMSNorm\texorpdfstring{ (Proposition~\ref*{prop:ln})}{}}\label{app:ln}

The reduction of LayerNorm to RMSNorm is standard: the centering folds into the adjacent weights \citep{elhage2021circuits}, and centering every write gives an RMSNorm network whose hidden states are mean-free \citep{ashkboos2024slicegpt,theus2025glmc}. We state it with the constants that the counts need. It is used in \cref{sec:setup,sec:norm} and for ViT-B in \cref{sec:skipless,app:scale}.

\begin{proposition}[LayerNorm reduces to RMSNorm]\label{prop:ln}
Let $Z = I - \tfrac1\dm\mathbf 1\mathbf 1^\top$ and $\mathcal V = \mathbf 1^\perp$, and consider a pre-LayerNorm network, skipless or residual, whose reads and readout see $\mathrm{LN}(h) = \gamma\odot hZ/\sqrt{\norm{hZ}^2/\dm + \varepsilon} + \beta$, with $h$, $\gamma$ and $\beta$ row vectors.\\
\textup{(a)} Replacing each read $W$ by $\mathrm{diag}(\gamma)W$ with the added bias $\beta W$, and each write $W$, write bias $b$ and embedding $E$ by $WZ$, $bZ$ and $EZ$, gives a network that computes the same function, whose hidden states lie in $\mathcal V$, and whose reads see $N(h) = h/\sqrt{\norm{h}^2/\dm + \varepsilon}$.\\
\textup{(b)} In an orthonormal basis of $\mathcal V$, this network is a pre-RMSNorm network of width $\dm-1$, with every read multiplied by $\sqrt{\dm/(\dm-1)}$ and $\varepsilon$ replaced by $\varepsilon \dm/(\dm-1)$.\\
\textup{(c)} If the gains $\gamma$ have nonzero entries, the difference in redundancy between the skipless and the residual version of the LayerNorm network equals the difference for the reduced networks of~(b).
\end{proposition}

\para{Overview.} LayerNorm sees only the mean-free part of the stream, so the mean can be removed from every write without changing the function. On the mean-free subspace LayerNorm is RMSNorm up to constants. The map from the parameters of the LayerNorm network to those of the reduced network has a derivative that is onto, and it is the same for the skipless and the residual version, so it preserves the difference in redundancy.

\begin{proof}
(a) Since $\mathrm{LN}(h) = \gamma\odot N(hZ) + \beta$, a read satisfies $\mathrm{LN}(h)\,W = N(hZ)\,\mathrm{diag}(\gamma)W + \beta W$. Every hidden state is a sum of writes, directly in a skipless network and through the skips in a residual one, so replacing every write, write bias and the embedding by its image under $Z$ replaces every hidden state $h$ by $hZ$. Since $Z^2 = Z$, every read then sees the same $N(hZ)$ as before, and the function is unchanged; the hidden states lie in $\mathcal V$.
(b) Let the columns of $\bar U \in \R^{\dm\times(\dm-1)}$ be an orthonormal basis of $\mathcal V$ and $h = y\bar U^\top$ with a row vector $y$. Then $\norm{h}^2/\dm + \varepsilon = \tfrac{\dm-1}{\dm}\big(\norm{y}^2/(\dm-1) + \varepsilon \dm/(\dm-1)\big)$, so $N(y\bar U^\top) = \sqrt{\dm/(\dm-1)}\,N'(y)\bar U^\top$, where $N'$ is RMSNorm of dimension $\dm-1$ with constant $\varepsilon \dm/(\dm-1)$. A read $N(y\bar U^\top)W$ is the read $\sqrt{\dm/(\dm-1)}\,\bar U^\top W$ of $N'(y)$, and a write $WZ$ is the write $W\bar U$ in the coordinates $y$.
(c) The reduced parameters are $\sqrt{\dm/(\dm-1)}\,\bar U^\top\mathrm{diag}(\gamma)W$ and $b + \beta W$ for each read, $W\bar U$ and $b\bar U$ for each write, $E\bar U$ for the embedding, and the analogous expressions for the readout. For $\gamma$ with nonzero entries each is linear and onto in $W$, $b$ or $E$, so the derivative of the map from the parameters of the LayerNorm network to those of the reduced network is onto. The LayerNorm network computes the reduced network's function composed with this map, so by the chain rule every batch Jacobian of the LayerNorm network is that of the reduced network times this derivative, and at corresponding parameters both have the same functional dimension, and the redundancy of the LayerNorm network is that of the reduced network plus the difference of the parameter counts. That difference is the same for the skipless and the residual versions, which have the same shapes.
\end{proof}

Section~6 of the notebook (\cref{app:checks}) checks each step on a residual pre-LayerNorm network with $\dm = 6$: folding the gains changes the function by a relative $2\times10^{-16}$, centering the writes by $2\times10^{-16}$, and an orthogonal change of basis on $\mathcal V$ by $8\times10^{-16}$, while a generic orthogonal change that moves $\mathbf 1$ changes it by $0.75$.

\subsection{RMSNorm: a gauge fix and a constraint\texorpdfstring{ (Theorem~\ref*{thm:rms})}{}}\label{app:prf-rms}

We recall \cref{thm:rms} from \cref{sec:norm}, where $\Pi_\ell = A_1\cdots A_\ell$, with $\Pi_0 = I$.
\thmRMS*

\para{Overview.} Part (a) runs the forward sweep of \cpref{thm:gauge}{a} with orthogonal changes of basis only, which remove the orthogonal factor of each skip and leave its triangular or symmetric factor. Part (b) reruns the change of variables of \cpref{thm:gauge}{a} and locates the only step that can fail, the read through $N$; \cref{lem:rms} says exactly when a matrix can be moved through $N$. Part (c) counts the symmetries with \cref{prop:junction} and \cref{cor:count}. Part (d) checks that these steps hold for $\varepsilon = 0$.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:rms}]
\emph{Part (a).} A change of basis $G_\ell \in \Ort(\dm)$ at $h_\ell$ preserves the function (\cpref{prop:junction}{b}). It multiplies the writes into $h_\ell$, the skip $A_\ell$ included, on the right by $G_\ell$, and the reads of $h_\ell$ on the left by $G_\ell^{-1}$: the raw read $A_{\ell+1}$ directly, and the reads through $N$ because $N(hG_\ell) = N(h)G_\ell$ (\cref{lem:rms}). Define $Q_\ell$ recursively, with $Q_0 = I$: write $Q_{\ell-1}^\top A_\ell = R_\ell Q_\ell^\top$, an RQ factorization with $R_\ell$ upper triangular with positive diagonal and $Q_\ell$ orthogonal, which exists because $Q_{\ell-1}^\top A_\ell$ is invertible, and is unique. With $G_0 = I$ and $G_\ell = Q_\ell$ the skip $A_\ell$ becomes $Q_{\ell-1}^\top A_\ell Q_\ell = R_\ell$. The same computation with the polar decomposition $Q_{\ell-1}^\top A_\ell = S_\ell Q_\ell^\top$ in place of the RQ factorization gives $Q_{\ell-1}^\top A_\ell Q_\ell = S_\ell$: every invertible matrix $X$ is $X = SQ^\top$ with $S = (XX^\top)^{1/2}$ symmetric positive definite and $Q^\top = S^{-1}X$ orthogonal, uniquely. The readout absorbs the last factor, $D \mapsto Q_L^\top D$. The recursion can also start at any $h_k$ with any orthogonal $Q_k$, running forward as above for $\ell > k$ and backward for $\ell \le k$ with the QR factorization $A_\ell Q_\ell = Q_{\ell-1}R_\ell$ or the polar decomposition $A_\ell Q_\ell = Q_{\ell-1}S_\ell$; the embedding absorbs $Q_0$. With $Q_k$ the rotation of \cpref{prop:normalform}{a} for one block of a read or a write of $h_k$, that block becomes triangular too, and $(L+1)\dm(\dm-1)/2$ parameters are removed in all. If $Q_0$ and $Q_L$ are fixed, as with a tied readout, the recursions from $h_0$ forward and from $h_L$ backward meet at any one skip, which keeps its full matrix; without normalization the same holds in $\GL(\dm)$, with identities in place of the $R_\ell$.

\emph{Part (b).} Let the network be $h_\ell = h_{\ell-1}A_\ell + f_\ell(N(h_{\ell-1})\Wread^{\ell})\Wwrite^{\ell}$ and set $h'_\ell = h_\ell\Pi_\ell^{-1}$, the forward version of \cpref{thm:gauge}{a}, which turns every skip into the identity. The read of block $\ell$ in the new variables is $N(h'_{\ell-1}\Pi_{\ell-1})\Wread^{\ell}$. If every $\Pi_\ell$, $\ell = 0, \dots, L$, lies in $\G$, write $\Pi_\ell = c_\ell\hat Q_\ell$ with $\hat Q_\ell$ orthogonal and $c_\ell = 1$ when $\varepsilon > 0$; Lemma~\ref{lem:rms} gives $N(h'\Pi_{\ell-1}) = N(h')\hat Q_{\ell-1}$, so $\Wread'^{\ell} = \hat Q_{\ell-1}\Wread^{\ell}$ together with $\Wwrite'^{\ell} = \Wwrite^{\ell}\Pi_\ell^{-1}$ and $D' = \hat Q_LD$, since the readout reads $N(h_L)$, reproduces the induction of Theorem~\rpref{thm:gauge}{a}, and the result is a pre-RMSNorm residual network. If some $\Pi_{\ell-1}$ is not in $\G$, Lemma~\ref{lem:rms} says there is no matrix $M$ with $N(h'\Pi_{\ell-1}) = N(h')M$ for all $h'$; since the read has rank $\dm$, the read of block $\ell$ is then not of the form $N(h')W$ for any $W$, and the same holds for the readout and $\Pi_L$, so the change of variables does not yield a pre-RMSNorm residual network. As in the proof of \cpref{thm:gauge}{a}, every other change of variables that turns the skips into identities differs from this one by a global $K$, which must lie in $\G$ because $\Pi_0 = I$, and so leads to the same conclusion.
